Used by\n132\t      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n133\t    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n134\t      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n135\t    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n136\t      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n137\t    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n138\t      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n139\t      report. The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n152\t    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n153\t    \"\"\"\n154\t\n155\t    name: str\n156\t    passed: bool\n157\t    message: str = \"\"\n158\t    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n159\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n160\t    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n161\t\n162\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n163\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n164\t\n165\t        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n166\t        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n167\t        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n168\t        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n169\t        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n170\t        \"\"\"\n171\t\n172\t        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n173\t        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n174\t        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n175\t        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n176\t        # needed here. The 8-field\n201\t    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n202\t\n203\t    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n204\t      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n205\t      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n206\t    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n207\t      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n208\t      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n209\t    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n210\t      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n211\t    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n212\t      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n213\t      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n214\t    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n215\t      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n216\t    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n217\t      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n218\t      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n219\t    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n220\t      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n221\t      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n222\t      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n223\t    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n224\t      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n225\t      clean diff rather than an exception.\n226\t\n227\t    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n228\t    records mid-render. The Reporter uses this to group\n308\t      identical harness failures across runs.\n309\t\n310\t    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n311\t    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n312\t    \"\"\"\n313\t\n314\t    eval_id: str\n315\t    title: str\n316\t    status: EvalStatus\n317\t    duration_sec: float\n318\t    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n319\t    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n320\t    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n321\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n322\t    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n323\t\n324\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n325\t        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n326\t        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n327\t        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n328\t        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n329\t        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n330\t        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n331\t        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n332\t            raise ValueError(\n333\t                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n334\t                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n335\t            )\n336\t\n337\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n338\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n339\t\n340\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n341\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n342\t        internals. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n382\t    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n383\t    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n384\t    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n385\t    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n386\t\n387\t    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n388\t      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n389\t      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n390\t    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n391\t      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n392\t      cannot be swapped after construction.\n393\t    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n394\t      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n395\t      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n396\t      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n397\t    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n398\t      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n399\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n400\t      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n401\t      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n402\t      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n403\t      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n404\t      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n405\t    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n406\t      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n407\t      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n408\t    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n409\t      as ``stdout``.\n410\t    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n411\t      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n412\t      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n413\t      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n414\t      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n415\t    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n416\t      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n417\t      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n418\t      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n419\t      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n420\t\n421\t    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n422\t    stable, reviewable JSON.\n423\t    \"\"\"\n424\t\n425\t    eval_id: str\n426\t    outcome: EvalOutcome\n427\t    start: str\n428\t    end: str\n429\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n430\t    stdout: str = \"\"\n431\t    stderr: str = \"\"\n432\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n433\t    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n434\t\n435\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n436\t        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n437\t        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n438\t        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n439\t        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n440\t        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n441\t        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n442\t        if self.start and self.end:\n443\t            computed = (\n444\t                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n445\t            ).total_seconds()\n446\t            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n447\t            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n448\t            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n449\t\n450\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n451\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n452\t\n453\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n454\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n455\t        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n456\t        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n457\t        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n458\t        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n459\t        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n460\t        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n461\t        \"\"\"\n462\t\n463\t        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n464\t        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n465\t            value = getattr(self, name)\n466\t            if name == \"outcome\":\n467\t                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n468\t            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n469\t                payload[name] = dict(value)\n470\t            elif name == \"error\":\n471\t                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n472\t            else:\n473\t                payload[name] = value\n474\t        return payload\n475\t\n476\t\n477\tdef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n478\t    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n479\t\n480\t    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n","numLines":492,"startLine":1,"totalLines":492}}}

Catching this\n169\t    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n170\t    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n171\t    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n172\t    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n173\t    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n174\t\n175\t    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n176\t    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n177\t    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n178\t    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n179\t    once it lands.\n180\t\n181\t    Attributes:\n182\t        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n183\t            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n184\t            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). The re-check closes that\n238\t       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n239\t    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n240\t       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n241\t       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n242\t       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n243\t       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n244\t       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n245\t       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n246\t       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n247\t       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n248\t       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n249\t       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n250\t       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n251\t    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n252\t       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n253\t       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n254\t       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n255\t       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n256\t       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n257\t       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n258\t       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n259\t       check would have passed.\n260\t\n261\t    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n262\t    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n263\t    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n264\t    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n265\t    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n266\t\n267\t    Args:\n268\t        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n269\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Must exist on disk; the\n270\t            ``strict=True`` resolve enforces this so a caller cannot\n271\t            invoke the guard \"ahead\" of ``mkdtemp``.\n272\t        scratch_root: Scratch root under which ``home_path`` must\n273\t            reside. Need not exist (``strict=False`` resolution); for\n274\t            test ergonomics callers can pass a path that\n275\t            :class:`HomeIsolation` will create via\n276\t            ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` shortly after.\n277\t        eval_id: The eval identifier the per-eval HOME corresponds to.\n278\t            Re-validated through :func:`validate_eval_id`.\n279\t        config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n280\t            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n281\t            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n282\t            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n283\t            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n284\t            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n285\t            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n286\t            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n287\t\n288\t    Raises:\n289\t        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. Catching this\n    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n\n    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n    once it lands.\n\n    Attributes:\n        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). Lets reporters bucket failures\n            without parsing the human-readable message.\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME path the guard was asked to\n            validate. Stored verbatim as supplied by the caller (no\n            resolution) so forensics keep the original input.\n        scratch_root: The declared scratch root the guard checked\n            ``home_path`` against. Stored verbatim.\n        eval_id: The eval id under inspection. Stored verbatim\n            (``repr``-rendered) so non-string smuggled scalars are\n            visible in reporter output.\n        detail: Free-form one-line message describing the specific\n            failure. Inherits from the underlying cause when one\n            exists (e.g. the ``ScratchRootViolation`` rendering).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        check: str,\n        home_path: Path,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        eval_id: object,\n        detail: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.check = check\n        self.home_path = home_path\n        self.scratch_root = scratch_root\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.detail = detail\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"FR-ISO2 containment check {check!r} failed for \"\n            f\"eval_id={eval_id!r} home_path={home_path!s} \"\n            f\"scratch_root={scratch_root!s}: {detail}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef containment_guard(\n    home_path: Path,\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    eval_id: str,\n    *,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-ISO2 three-check defense-in-depth guard.\n\n    The guard layers three independent checks on top of the per-eval\n    HOME materialization that :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` just performed.\n    Each check has a distinct attack vector in scope:\n\n    1. **eval_id regex** — re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n       The loader (T01.07) already validates the id pre-expand and\n       post-expand, but a caller bypassing :class:`SuiteLoader` (e.g.,\n       a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point) could construct\n       a :class:`HomeIsolation` directly. The re-check closes that\n       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n       check would have passed.\n\n    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n\n    Args:\n        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Must exist on disk; the\n            ``strict=True`` resolve enforces this so a caller cannot\n            invoke the guard \"ahead\" of ``mkdtemp``.\n        scratch_root: Scratch root under which ``home_path`` must\n            reside. Need not exist (``strict=False`` resolution); for\n            test ergonomics callers can pass a path that\n            :class:`HomeIsolation` will create via\n            ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` shortly after.\n        eval_id: The eval identifier the per-eval HOME corresponds to.\n            Re-validated through :func:`validate_eval_id`.\n        config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n\n    Raises:\n        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. The\n            ``check`` attribute identifies which one; ``detail`` carries\n            the human-readable cause; ``__cause__`` holds the underlying\n            exception when applicable.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Check 1: eval_id regex. Loader-bypass defense.\n    try:\n        validate_eval_id(eval_id)\n    except InvalidEvalId as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"eval_id\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=str(exc),\n        ) from exc\n\n    # Check 2: scratch-root allowlist (symlink-aware via resolve_scratch_root).\n    # ``config`` is required: see docstring rationale (NFR-SEC2 bypass close).\n    try:\n        resolved_scratch = resolve_scratch_root(scratch_root, config=config)\n    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=str(exc),\n        ) from exc\n\n    # Check 3: post-mkdtemp symlink resolution + containment under\n    # scratch_root. strict=True asserts the HOME exists (i.e., mkdtemp\n    # already ran). Any symlink inside home_path is collapsed; the\n    # resolved form must equal or live beneath the resolved scratch root.\n    try:\n        resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=True)\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"home_path_resolution\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=(\n                \"home_path does not exist on disk; FR-ISO2 requires the \"\n                \"guard to run AFTER mkdtemp materialized the per-eval \"\n                \"HOME\"\n            ),\n        ) from exc\n\n    if not (\n        resolved_home == resolved_scratch\n        or resolved_home.is_relative_to(resolved_scratch)\n    ):\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"home_path_escape\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=(\n                f\"resolved home_path {resolved_home!s} escapes resolved \"","numLines":250,"startLine":100,"totalLines":697}}}

The\n505\t        tag distinguishes harness bugs / environmental refusal (status\n506\t        ERRORED — tag present, no eval output) from real eval failures\n507\t        (status FAIL — eval ran, expectations missed). Parallel evals never collide on directory\n        names; mutating one HOME cannot affect siblings because each\n        HOME is its own ``mkdtemp`` root.\n\n        After ``mkdtemp`` succeeds, :func:`containment_guard` (FR-ISO2,\n        T02.08) is applied AFTER the per-eval HOME has been\n        materialized and BEFORE any hook deploy or eval-state write.\n        The guard re-validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 regex,\n        re-applies the AC12 scratch-root allowlist, and\n        ``Path.resolve(strict=True)``-checks the freshly created HOME\n        against the scratch root. Any failure surfaces as\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`; the partial per-eval HOME is\n        left on disk so callers can route teardown through\n        ``teardown(keep=True)`` for forensic inspection (the\n        containment exception itself carries the full forensic\n        payload — see NFR-ISO2 contract below).\n\n        Idempotency rule: calling :meth:`setup` twice on the same\n        instance raises :class:`RuntimeError`. This prevents the silent\n        directory leak that would otherwise occur if a caller looped\n        over an instance, and it makes the atomic-setup wrapper\n        (T02.13) easier to reason about.\n\n        Args:\n            config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Production callers\n                (orchestrator T03.16) pass the run's resolved config;\n                tests build an :class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist\n                includes the test scratch root.\n\n        Returns:\n            The freshly created per-eval HOME path (also recorded on\n            :attr:`home_path`).\n\n        Raises:\n            RuntimeError: If :meth:`setup` was already called on this\n                instance and :meth:`teardown` has not run since.\n            HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three FR-ISO2 checks\n                failed; the per-eval HOME directory was created and is\n                preserved on disk for inspection with a\n                ``setup_failed`` tag (NFR-ISO2 — see below).\n\n        NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract (T02.13 / D-0033)\n        ------------------------------------------------\n\n        Any exception raised AFTER :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` and BEFORE\n        ``setup`` returns successfully MUST leave the per-eval HOME on\n        disk. The private ``_home_path`` slot stays populated so a\n        caller that wraps ``setup`` in ``try/finally`` can route\n        teardown through ``teardown(keep=True)`` to preserve the\n        partial HOME for forensics.\n\n        Non-containment exceptions additionally drop a ``setup_failed``\n        artifact tag at ``<home>/.eval-meta/setup_failed`` (constant\n        :data:`SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH`). The tag's first line is the\n        exception class name; the body is the formatted traceback. The\n        tag distinguishes harness bugs / environmental refusal (status\n        ERRORED — tag present, no eval output) from real eval failures\n        (status FAIL — eval ran, expectations missed). The tag write\n        is best-effort: a secondary exception during tag write is\n        discarded so the original exception is the one the caller\n        sees. The original exception type is preserved verbatim — the\n        wrapper adds the tag side effect, it does not re-wrap or\n        re-classify the exception.\n\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` is the deliberate exception\n        to the tag-write rule: writing under a HOME the guard just\n        refused could land under the real ``~/.claude/`` directory\n        when the scratch root or the ``mkdtemp`` result is symlinked\n        there (the attack matrix exercised by NFR-SEC3 in T02.10).\n        The violation exception itself is the forensic signal — its\n        ``check``, ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id`` and\n        ``detail`` attributes carry everything a reporter needs to\n        bucket the eval as ERRORED.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._home_path is not None:  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                f\"HomeIsolation.setup() already called for eval_id={self.eval_id!r}\"\n            )\n\n        # Ensure the scratch root exists. ``parents=True, exist_ok=True``\n        # is safe because the FR-ISO2 guard below catches any path that\n        # resolves outside the policy allowlist.\n        self.home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        home = Path(\n            tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))\n        )\n        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", home)\n\n        # NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper. Anything that raises beyond this\n        # line — FR-ISO2 containment_guard today, any future post-mkdtemp\n        # work (hook deploy verification, capability stamping) tomorrow —\n        # leaves the per-eval HOME on disk so ``teardown(keep=True)``\n        # preserves the directory for post-mortem inspection without a\n        # re-resolve. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LwJoGygEFfponyaSFAeseK","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0046 — EvalResult implementation notes\n2\t\n3\t## Design decisions\n4\t\n5\t### Frozen dataclass\n6\t\n7\tT03.02 only says \"EvalResult dataclass\" (T03.01 explicitly says \"frozen\", T03.03 explicitly says \"frozen\"). We chose **frozen** anyway to match the rest of the eval-models surface: every record the Reporter consumes is immutable so consumers cannot mutate cached state mid-render. `__post_init__` uses `object.__setattr__` to overwrite `duration_sec`, which is the documented escape hatch for frozen dataclasses.\n8\t\n9\t### Timestamp representation\n10\t\n11\tDM-003 enumerates `start` / `end` without prescribing a Python type. The task **Steps** explicitly choose ISO 8601 strings (Step 2: \"Choose datetime representation (ISO 8601 strings)\"). Rationale:\n12\t\n13\t- The values round-trip through `json.dumps` with no custom encoder.\n14\t- The Reporter forwards them verbatim into `summary.json`.\n15\t- We still need duration arithmetic, which is handled by `datetime.fromisoformat(...)` at `__post_init__` time. Python 3.11+ `fromisoformat` accepts all reasonable ISO 8601 variants; the project targets `>=3.10` and uses 3.12 in CI.\n16\t\n17\t### `duration_sec` overwrite vs. validate\n18\t\n19\tWe considered three options for keeping `duration_sec` consistent with timestamps:\n20\t\n21\t1. **Validate** (raise `ValueError` if `duration != end - start`). Rejected — too strict for the partial-summary path on SIGINT, where the runner may not have written `end` yet.\n22\t2. **Overwrite unconditionally**. Rejected — destroys the partial-summary use case entirely.\n23\t3. **Overwrite when both timestamps are non-empty, otherwise honour the caller's value** (chosen). This satisfies the \"consistently\" wording in the AC for happy-path runs, while keeping the partial summary path constructible. Both behaviours are explicitly tested (`test_eval_result_duration_sec_caller_value_is_overwritten` and `test_eval_result_duration_sec_kept_when_timestamps_missing`).\n24\t\n25\t### `error` serialisation shape\n26\t\n27\tDM-003 types `error` as `Optional[Exception]`. Live `BaseException` instances do not pass through `json.dumps`, so `to_dict()` must lower them into a JSON-safe shape. We chose `{\"type\": \"<fully.qualified.ClassName>\", \"message\": str(error)}` over the alternatives:\n28\t\n29\t- **`str(error)` only.** Rejected — loses the exception class, which the Reporter uses to group identical harness failures across runs (mirroring the `error_class` rationale in DM-001).\n30\t- **`repr(error)`.** Rejected — repr formatting drifts across Python versions and exception subclasses.\n31\t- **Include the full traceback.** Rejected — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source; DM-003's `error` is the Reporter-facing summary handle, not a forensics record.\n32\t\n33\tThe implementation lives in `_render_error()`; `dataclasses.asdict` is intentionally **not** used because it would attempt to recurse into the exception instance.\n34\t\n35\t### Why both `EvalOutcome` *and* `EvalResult` exist\n36\t\n37\tDM-001 (EvalOutcome) and DM-003 (EvalResult) intentionally separate two concerns:\n38\t\n39\t- **EvalOutcome** — the *assertions-and-status* record the runner emits. Drives exit-code logic and the N′-vs-K invariant guard (FR-RPT1 / T03.11).\n40\t- **EvalResult** — the *stdout/stderr-and-timing* envelope the Reporter consumes. Carries the captured streams, lifecycle timestamps, and harness-level error.\n41\t\n42\tThe pair travels together: a runner finishes its lifecycle, builds an `EvalOutcome`, then wraps it in an `EvalResult` with the captured I/O and timing. The Reporter never reads them independently.\n43\t\n44\t## Future work touchpoints\n45\t\n46\t- **T03.04 / T03.05 (EvalRunner)** — will be the construction site for `EvalResult`. The runner must capture monotonic timestamps in ISO 8601 and feed them in.\n47\t- **T03.13 (Reporter)** — will consume `EvalResult.to_dict()` per row of `evals[]` in `summary.json` (DM-012 / T03.10 defines the surrounding schema).\n48\t- **T03.15 (RunOrchestrator)** — will collect one `EvalResult` per expanded spec from the worker pool and forward them to the Reporter alongside the `RunSummary` (DM-004 / T03.09).\n49\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"35cb7f75-82ca-46f2-bae8-dfd94d2ab673","uuid":"5ca20523-bdb5-4826-82d8-74d119428141","timestamp":"2026-05-20T11:07:40.146Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0046/notes.md","content":"# D-0046 — EvalResult implementation notes\n\n## Design decisions\n\n### Frozen dataclass\n\nT03.02 only says \"EvalResult dataclass\" (T03.01 explicitly says \"frozen\", T03.03 explicitly says \"frozen\"). We chose **frozen** anyway to match the rest of the eval-models surface: every record the Reporter consumes is immutable so consumers cannot mutate cached state mid-render. `__post_init__` uses `object.__setattr__` to overwrite `duration_sec`, which is the documented escape hatch for frozen dataclasses.\n\n### Timestamp representation\n\nDM-003 enumerates `start` / `end` without prescribing a Python type. The task **Steps** explicitly choose ISO 8601 strings (Step 2: \"Choose datetime representation (ISO 8601 strings)\"). Rationale:\n\n- The values round-trip through `json.dumps` with no custom encoder.\n- The Reporter forwards them verbatim into `summary.json`.\n- We still need duration arithmetic, which is handled by `datetime.fromisoformat(...)` at `__post_init__` time. Python 3.11+ `fromisoformat` accepts all reasonable ISO 8601 variants; the project targets `>=3.10` and uses 3.12 in CI.\n\n### `duration_sec` overwrite vs. validate\n\nWe considered three options for keeping `duration_sec` consistent with timestamps:\n\n1. **Validate** (raise `ValueError` if `duration != end - start`). Rejected — too strict for the partial-summary path on SIGINT, where the runner may not have written `end` yet.\n2. **Overwrite unconditionally**. Rejected — destroys the partial-summary use case entirely.\n3. **Overwrite when both timestamps are non-empty, otherwise honour the caller's value** (chosen). This satisfies the \"consistently\" wording in the AC for happy-path runs, while keeping the partial summary path constructible. Both behaviours are explicitly tested (`test_eval_result_duration_sec_caller_value_is_overwritten` and `test_eval_result_duration_sec_kept_when_timestamps_missing`).\n\n### `error` serialisation shape\n\nDM-003 types `error` as `Optional[Exception]`. Live `BaseException` instances do not pass through `json.dumps`, so `to_dict()` must lower them into a JSON-safe shape. We chose `{\"type\": \"<fully.qualified.ClassName>\", \"message\": str(error)}` over the alternatives:\n\n- **`str(error)` only.** Rejected — loses the exception class, which the Reporter uses to group identical harness failures across runs (mirroring the `error_class` rationale in DM-001).\n- **`repr(error)`.** Rejected — repr formatting drifts across Python versions and exception subclasses.\n- **Include the full traceback.** Rejected — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source; DM-003's `error` is the Reporter-facing summary handle, not a forensics record.\n\nThe implementation lives in `_render_error()`; `dataclasses.asdict` is intentionally **not** used because it would attempt to recurse into the exception instance.\n\n### Why both `EvalOutcome` *and* `EvalResult` exist\n\nDM-001 (EvalOutcome) and DM-003 (EvalResult) intentionally separate two concerns:\n\n- **EvalOutcome** — the *assertions-and-status* record the runner emits. Drives exit-code logic and the N′-vs-K invariant guard (FR-RPT1 / T03.11).\n- **EvalResult** — the *stdout/stderr-and-timing* envelope the Reporter consumes. Carries the captured streams, lifecycle timestamps, and harness-level error.\n\nThe pair travels together: a runner finishes its lifecycle, builds an `EvalOutcome`, then wraps it in an `EvalResult` with the captured I/O and timing. The Reporter never reads them independently.\n\n## Future work touchpoints\n\n- **T03.04 / T03.05 (EvalRunner)** — will be the construction site for `EvalResult`. The runner must capture monotonic timestamps in ISO 8601 and feed them in.\n- **T03.13 (Reporter)** — will consume `EvalResult.to_dict()` per row of `evals[]` in `summary.json` (DM-012 / T03.10 defines the surrounding schema).\n- **T03.15 (RunOrchestrator)** — will collect one `EvalResult` per expanded spec from the worker pool and forward them to the Reporter alongside the `RunSummary` (DM-004 / T03.09).\n","numLines":49,"startLine":1,"totalLines":49}}}

Every\n``ExpectCallable`` (COMP-010 / T01.14 stubs in M1, primitives land in M4)\nreturns one of these so the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) can render per-\nExpect outcomes without re-parsing assertion messages.\n\n``ExpectFailure`` carries the 8 fields declared by DM-005: ``eval_id``,\n``expect_id``, ``expect_name``, ``expected``, ``actual``, ``message``,\n``artifact_ref``, ``traceback``. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) renders\nexactly one ``ExpectFailure`` per failing Expect inside ``EvalOutcome``\n(T03.01).\n\nField names match the schema verbatim so round-tripping a manifest entry\nthrough ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` is deterministic. Sequence fields are stored\nas tuples so equality is preserved and instances cannot be mutated through\ntheir container (the dataclass itself is also frozen).\n\nParameterize expansion and FR-SCH2 id re-validation happen one layer up in\nCOMP-002 (T01.07); this module is intentionally schema-shape-only.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport dataclasses\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom typing import Any, Literal, Mapping, Optional, get_args\n\n\nEvalStatus = Literal[\n    \"PASS\",\n    \"FAIL\",\n    \"ERRORED\",\n    \"TIMEOUT\",\n    \"INTERRUPTED\",\n    \"SKIPPED\",\n    \"XFAIL\",\n    \"XPASS\",\n]\n# Resolved tuple for runtime membership checks. Kept in module scope so the\n# Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and orchestrator can validate status values\n# against the same authoritative set without re-deriving from the Literal.\nEVAL_STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(EvalStatus)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalSpec:\n    \"\"\"Parsed manifest entry (one row of ``evals[]``).\n\n    Field shape mirrors ``evalEntry`` in ``suites/suite.schema.json``:\n\n    * ``id`` / ``title`` are required by the schema.\n    * ``category`` defaults to an empty string for unclassified evals.\n    * ``requires`` is the tuple of capability names a runner must check.\n    * ``timeout_sec`` is ``None`` when the manifest does not override the\n      suite-wide default in ``EvalConfig.defaults``.\n    * ``isolation`` carries the raw ``isolationBlock`` mapping; ``None``\n      when omitted.\n    * ``inputs`` / ``expects`` / ``parameterize`` are tuples of raw row\n      mappings. Their interior shape is validated by ``suite.schema.json``;\n      richer typed models land later (ExpectCallable in M4).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    id: str\n    title: str\n    category: str = \"\"\n    requires: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n    timeout_sec: int | None = None\n    isolation: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None\n    inputs: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    expects: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    parameterize: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_dict(cls, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> \"EvalSpec\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalSpec`` from a schema-valid manifest entry.\n\n        The mapping keys mirror ``suite.schema.json`` field names. Sequence\n        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n        constrained their shape).\n\n        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n        contract bug rather than user error.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n            )\n\n        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n\n        return cls(\n            id=data[\"id\"],\n            title=data[\"title\"],\n            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectResult:\n    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n\n    Six-field contract:\n\n    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n      default for passing results.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n\n    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    passed: bool\n    message: str = \"\"\n    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n\n        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"expect_id\",\n    \"expect_name\",\n    \"expected\",\n    \"actual\",\n    \"message\",\n    \"artifact_ref\",\n    \"traceback\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectFailure:\n    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n\n    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    title: str\n    status: EvalStatus\n    duration_sec: float\n    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":37,"oldLines":9,"newStart":37,"newLines":14,"lines":[" import dataclasses"," from dataclasses import dataclass, field"," from datetime import datetime","-from typing import Any, Literal, Mapping, Optional, get_args","+from pathlib import Path","+from types import MappingProxyType","+from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Literal, Mapping, Optional, get_args"," ","+if TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - import guard for type-checkers only.","+    from .isolation import HomeIsolation"," ","+"," EvalStatus = Literal[","     \"PASS\",","     \"FAIL\","]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured.
